you always review first\n\n## Execution Workflow\n\nFollow these steps **in order** every time:\n\n### Step 1 — Scope Resolution\nDetermine what to scan:\n- If a path was provided (`/security-review src/auth/`), scan only that scope\n- If no path given, scan the **entire project** starting from the root\n- Identify the language(s) and framework(s) in use (check package.json, requirements.txt,\n  go.mod, Cargo.toml, pom.xml, Gemfile, composer.json, etc.)\n- Read `references/language-patterns.md` to load language-specific vulnerability patterns\n\n### Step 2 — Dependency Audit\nBefore scanning source code, audit dependencies first (fast wins):\n- **Node.js**: Check `package.json` + `package-lock.json` for known vulnerable packages\n- **Python**: Check `requirements.txt` / `pyproject.toml` / `Pipfile`\n- **Java**: Check `pom.xml` / `build.gradle`\n- **Ruby**: Check `Gemfile.lock`\n- **Rust**: Check `Cargo.toml`\n- **Go**: Check `go.sum`\n- Flag packages with known CVEs, deprecated crypto libs, or suspiciously old pinned versions\n- Read `references/vulnerable-packages.md` for a curated watchlist\n\n### Step 3 — Secrets & Exposure Scan\nScan ALL files (including config, env, CI/CD, Dockerfiles, IaC) for:\n- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, passwords, private keys\n- `.env` files accidentally committed\n- Secrets in comments or debug logs\n- Cloud credentials (AWS, GCP, Azure, Stripe, Twilio, etc.)\n- Database connection strings with credentials embedded\n- Read `references/secret-patterns.md` for regex patterns and entropy heuristics to apply\n\n### Step 4 — Vulnerability Deep Scan\nThis is the core scan. Reason about the code — don't just pattern-match.\nRead `references/vuln-categories.md` for full details on each category.\n\n**Injection Flaws**\n- SQL Injection: raw queries with string interpolation, ORM misuse, second-order SQLi\n- XSS: unescaped output, dangerouslySetInnerHTML, innerHTML, template injection\n- Command Injection: exec/spawn/system with user input\n- LDAP, XPath, Header, Log injection\n\n**Authentication & Access Control**\n- Missing authentication on sensitive endpoints\n- Broken object-level authorization (BOLA/IDOR)\n- JWT weaknesses (alg:none, weak secrets, no expiry validation)\n- Session fixation, missing CSRF protection\n- Privilege escalation paths\n- Mass assignment / parameter pollution\n\n**Data Handling**\n- Sensitive data in logs, error messages, or API responses\n- Missing encryption at rest or in transit\n- Insecure deserialization\n- Path traversal / directory traversal\n- XXE (XML External Entity) processing\n- SSRF (Server-Side Request Forgery)\n\n**Cryptography**\n- Use of MD5, SHA1, DES for security purposes\n- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n- Weak random number generation (Math.random() for tokens)\n- Missing TLS certificate validation\n\n**Business Logic**\n- Race conditions (TOCTOU)\n- Integer overflow in financial calculations\n- Missing rate limiting on sensitive endpoints\n- Predictable resource identifiers\n\n### Step 5 — Cross-File Data Flow Analysis\nAfter the per-file scan, perform a **holistic review**:\n- Trace user-controlled input from entry points (HTTP params, headers, body, file uploads)\n  all the way to sinks (DB queries, exec calls, HTML output, file writes)\n- Identify vulnerabilities that only appear when looking at multiple files together\n- Check for insecure trust boundaries between services or modules\n\n### Step 6 — Self-Verification Pass\nFor EACH finding:\n1. Re-read the relevant code with fresh eyes\n2. Ask: \"Is this actually exploitable, or is there sanitization I missed?\"\n3. Check if a framework or middleware already handles this upstream\n4. Downgrade or discard findings that aren't genuine vulnerabilities\n5. Assign final severity: CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW / INFO\n\n### Step 7 — Generate Security Report\nOutput the full report in the format defined in `references/report-format.md`.\n\n### Step 8 — Propose Patches\nFor every CRITICAL and HIGH finding, generate a concrete patch:\n- Show the vulnerable code (before)\n- Show the fixed code (after)\n- Explain what changed and why\n- Preserve the original code style, variable names, and structure\n- Add a comment explaining the fix inline\n\nExplicitly state: **\"Review each patch before applying. Entropy-Based Detection\n\nApply to string literals > 20 characters in assignment context.\nHigh entropy (Shannon entropy > 4.5 bits/char) + length > 20 = likely secret.\n\n```\nCalculate entropy: -sum(p * log2(p)) for each character frequency p\nThreshold: > 4.5 bits/char AND > 20 chars AND assigned to a variable\n```\n\nCommon false positives to exclude:\n- Lorem ipsum text\n- HTML/CSS content\n- Base64-encoded non-sensitive config (but flag and note)\n- UUID/GUID (entropy is high but format is recognizable)\n\n---\n\n## Files That Should Never Be Committed\n\nFlag if these files exist in the repo root or are tracked by git:\n```\n.env\n.env.local\n.env.production\n.env.staging\n*.pem\n*.key\n*.p12\n*.pfx\nid_rsa\nid_ed25519\ncredentials.json\nservice-account.json\ngcp-key.json\nsecrets.yaml\nsecrets.json\nconfig/secrets.yml\n```\n\nAlso check `.gitignore` — if a secret file pattern is NOT in .gitignore, flag it.\n\n---\n\n## CI/CD & IaC Secret Risks\n\n### GitHub Actions — flag these patterns:\n```yaml\n# Hardcoded values in env: blocks (should use ${{ secrets.NAME }})\nenv:\n  API_KEY:[REDACTED]   # VULNERABLE\n\n# Printing secrets\n- run: echo ${{ secrets.MY_SECRET }}   # leaks to logs\n```\n\n### Docker — flag these:\n```dockerfile\n# Secrets in ENV (persisted in image layers)\nENV AWS_SECRET_KEY=actual-value\n\n# Secrets passed as build args (visible in image history)\nARG API_KEY=[REDACTED]\n```\n\n### Terraform — flag these:\n```hcl\n# Hardcoded sensitive values (should use var or data source)\npassword=[REDACTED]\naccess_key = \"AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Safe Patterns (Do NOT flag)\n\nThese are intentional placeholders — recognize and skip:\n```\n\"your-api-key-here\"\n\"<YOUR_API_KEY>\"\n\"${API_KEY}\"\n\"${process.env.API_KEY}\"\n\"os.environ.get('API_KEY')\"\n\"REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_KEY\"\n\"xxx...xxx\"\n\"sk-...\" (in documentation/comments)\n```\n\n---\n[Tool result call_WEBbnT3N85qk9gnHLlSQFjWK]\n# Language-Specific Vulnerability Patterns\n\nLoad the relevant section during Step 1 (Scope Resolution) after execution\nFunction('return ...')   // same as eval\nchild_process.exec()     // command injection if user input reaches it\nfs.readFile              // path traversal if user controls path\nfs.writeFile             // path traversal if user controls path\n```\n\n### Express.js specific\n```js\n// Missing helmet (security headers)\nconst app = express()\n// Should have: app.use(helmet())\n\n// Body size limits missing (DoS)\napp.use(express.json())\n// Should have: app.use(express.json({ limit: '10kb' }))\n\n// CORS misconfiguration\napp.use(cors({ origin: '*' })) detection guidance for every vulnerability category.\nLoad this during Step 4 of the scan workflow.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Injection Flaws\n\n### SQL Injection\n**What to look for:**\n- String concatenation or interpolation inside SQL queries\n- Raw `.query()`, `.execute()`, `.raw()` calls with variables\n- ORM `whereRaw()`, `selectRaw()`, `orderByRaw()` with user input\n- Second-order SQLi: data stored safely, then used unsafely later\n- Stored procedures called with unsanitized input\n\n**Detection signals (all languages):**\n```\n\"SELECT ... \" + variable\n`SELECT ... ${variable}`\nf\"SELECT ... {variable}\"\n\"SELECT ... %s\" % variable   # Only safe with proper driver parameterization\ncursor.execute(\"... \" + input)\ndb.raw(`... ${req.params.id}`)\n```\n\n**Safe patterns (parameterized):**\n```js\ndb.query('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?', [userId])\nUser.findOne({ where: { id: userId } })  // ORM safe\n```\n\n**Escalation checkers:**\n- Is the query result ever used in another query? (second-order)\n- Is the table/column name user-controlled? (cannot be parameterized — must allowlist)\n\n---\n\n### Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)\n**What to look for:**\n- `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `document.write()` with user data\n- `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in React\n- Template engines rendering unescaped: `{{{ var }}}` (Handlebars), `!= var` (Pug)\n- jQuery `.html()`, `.append()` with user data\n- `eval()`, `setTimeout(string)`, `setInterval(string)` with user data\n- DOM-based: `location.hash`, `document.referrer`, `window.name` written to DOM\n- Stored XSS: user input saved to DB, rendered without escaping later\n\n**Detection by framework:**\n- **React**: Safe by default EXCEPT `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`\n- **Angular**: Safe by default EXCEPT `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`\n- **Vue**: Safe by default EXCEPT `v-html`\n- **Vanilla JS**: Every DOM write is suspect\n\n---\n\n### Command Injection\n**What to look for (Node.js):**\n```js\nexec(userInput)\nexecSync(`ping ${host}`)\nspawn('sh', ['-c', userInput])\nchild_process.exec('ls ' + dir)\n```\n\n**What to look for (Python):**\n```python\nos.system(user_input)\nsubprocess.call(user_input, shell=True)\neval(user_input)\n```\n\n**What to look for (PHP):**\n```php\nexec($input)\nsystem($_GET['cmd'])\npassthru($input)\n`$input`  # backtick operator\n```\n\n**Safe alternatives:** Use array form of spawn/subprocess without shell=True; use allowlists for commands.\n\n---\n\n### Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)\n**What to look for:**\n- HTTP requests where the URL is user-controlled\n- Webhooks, URL preview, image fetch features\n- PDF generators that fetch external URLs\n- Redirects to user-supplied URLs\n\n**High-risk targets:**\n- AWS metadata service: `169.254.169.254`\n- Internal services: `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `10.x.x.x`, `192.168.x.x`\n- Cloud metadata endpoints\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\nfetch(req.body.url)\naxios.get(userSuppliedUrl)\nhttp.get(params.webhook)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Authentication & Access Control\n\n### Broken Object Level Authorization (BOLA / IDOR)\n**What to look for:**\n- Resource IDs taken directly from URL/params without ownership check\n- `findById(req.params.id)` without verifying `userId === currentUser.id`\n- Numeric sequential IDs (easily guessable)\n\n**Example vulnerable pattern:**\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: no ownership check\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findById(req.params.id);\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n\n// SAFE: verify ownership\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findOne({ _id: req.params.id, owner: req.user.id });\n  if (!doc) return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Forbidden' });\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n### JWT Vulnerabilities\n**What to look for:**\n- `alg: \"none\"` accepted\n- Weak or hardcoded secrets: `secret`, `password`, `1234`\n- No expiry (`exp` claim) validation\n- Algorithm confusion (RS256 → HS256 downgrade)\n- JWT stored in `localStorage` (XSS risk; prefer httpOnly cookie)\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\njwt.verify(token, secret, { algorithms: ['HS256'] })  // Check algorithms array\njwt.decode(token)  // WARNING: decode does NOT verify signature\n```\n\n---\n\n### Missing Authentication / Authorization\n**What to look for:**\n- Admin or sensitive endpoints missing auth middleware\n- Routes defined after `app.use(authMiddleware)` vs before it\n- Feature flags or debug endpoints left exposed in production\n- GraphQL resolvers missing auth checks at field level\n\n---\n\n### CSRF\n**What to look for:**\n- State-changing operations (POST/PUT/DELETE) without CSRF token\n- APIs relying only on cookies for auth without SameSite attribute\n- Missing `SameSite=Strict` or `SameSite=Lax` on session cookies\n\n---\n\n## 3. Secrets & Sensitive Data Exposure\n\n### In-Code Secrets\nLook for patterns like:\n```\nAPI_KEY=[REDACTED]\npassword=[REDACTED]\nSECRET=[REDACTED]\nprivate_key = \"-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\"\naws_secret_access_key = \"wJalrXUtn...\"\n```\n\nEntropy heuristic: strings > 20 chars with high character variety in assignment context\nare likely secrets even if the variable name doesn't say so.\n\n### In Logs / Error Messages\n```js\nconsole.log('User password:[REDACTED], password)\nlogger.info({ user, token })   // token shouldn't be logged\nres.status(500).json({ error: err.stack })  // stack traces expose internals\n```\n\n### Sensitive Data in API Responses\n- Returning full user object including `password_hash`, `ssn`, `credit_card`\n- Including internal IDs or system paths in error responses\n\n---\n\n## 4. Cryptography\n\n### Weak Algorithms\n| Algorithm | Issue | Replace With |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| MD5 | Broken for security | SHA-256 or bcrypt (passwords) |\n| SHA-1 | Collision attacks | SHA-256 |\n| DES / 3DES | Weak key size | AES-256-GCM |\n| RC4 | Broken | AES-GCM |\n| ECB mode | No IV, patterns visible | GCM or CBC with random IV |\n\n### Weak Randomness\n```js\n// VULNERABLE\nMath.random()                    // not cryptographically secure\nDate.now()                       // predictable\nMath.random().toString(36)       // weak token generation\n\n// SAFE\ncrypto.randomBytes(32)           // Node.js\nsecrets.token_urlsafe(32)        // Python\n```\n\n### Password Hashing\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nhashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()\nhashlib.sha256(password.encode()).hexdigest()\n\n# SAFE\nbcrypt.hashpw(password, bcrypt.gensalt(rounds=12))\nargon2.hash(password)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Insecure Dependencies\n\n### What to flag:\n- Packages with known CVEs in installed version range\n- Packages abandoned > 2 years with no security updates\n- Packages with extremely broad permissions for their stated purpose\n- Transitive dependencies pulling in known-bad packages\n- Pinned versions that are significantly behind current (possible unpatched vulns)\n\n### High-risk package watchlist: see `references/vulnerable-packages.md`\n\n---\n\n## 6. Business Logic\n\n### Race Conditions (TOCTOU)\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: check then act without atomic lock\nconst balance = await getBalance(userId);\nif (balance >= amount) {\n  await deductBalance(userId, amount);  // race condition between check and deduct\n}\n\n// SAFE: use atomic DB transaction or optimistic locking\nawait db.transaction(async (trx) => {\n  const user = await User.query(trx).forUpdate().findById(userId);\n  if (user.balance < amount) throw new Error('Insufficient funds');\n  await user.$query(trx).patch({ balance: user.balance - amount });\n});\n```\n\n### Missing Rate Limiting\nFlag endpoints that:\n- Accept authentication credentials (login, 2FA)\n- Send emails or SMS\n- Perform expensive operations\n- Expose user enumeration (password reset, registration)\n\n---\n\n## 7. Path Traversal\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nfilename = request.args.get('file')\nwith open(f'/var/uploads/{filename}') as f:  # ../../../../etc/passwd\n\n# SAFE\nfilename = os.path.basename(request.args.get('file'))\nsafe_path = os.path.join('/var/uploads', filename)\nif not safe_path.startswith('/var/uploads/'):\n    abort(400)\n```\n\n---\n[Tool result call_lvsVr8rsz26rKjCKfSm2iLWE]\n# Vulnerable & High-Risk Package Watchlist\n\nLoad this during Step 2 (Dependency Audit). Resolve relative resource paths from that directory; non-conflicting root-level copies are legacy aliases only.\n\n---\nname: security-review\ndescription: 'AI-powered codebase security scanner that reasons about code like a security researcher — tracing data flows, understanding component interactions, and catching vulnerabilities that pattern-matching tools miss. Use this skill when asked to scan code for security vulnerabilities, find bugs, check for SQL injection, XSS, command injection, exposed API keys, hardcoded secrets, insecure dependencies, access control issues, or any request like \"is my code secure?\", \"review for security issues\", \"audit this codebase\", or \"check for vulnerabilities\". Covers injection flaws, authentication and access control bugs, secrets exposure, weak cryptography, insecure dependencies, and business logic issues across JavaScript, TypeScript, Python, Java, PHP, Go, Ruby, and Rust.'\n---\n\n# Security Review\n\nAn AI-powered security scanner that reasons about your codebase the way a human security\nresearcher would — tracing data flows, understanding component interactions, and catching\nvulnerabilities that pattern-matching tools miss.\n\n## When to Use This Skill\n\nUse this skill when the request involves:\n\n- Scanning a codebase or file for security vulnerabilities\n- Running a security review or vulnerability check\n- Checking for SQL injection, XSS, command injection, or other injection flaws\n- Finding exposed API keys, hardcoded secrets, or credentials in code\n- Auditing dependencies for known CVEs\n- Reviewing authentication, authorization, or access control logic\n- Detecting insecure cryptography or weak randomness\n- Performing a data flow analysis to trace user input to dangerous sinks\n- Any request phrasing like \"is my code secure?\", \"scan this file\", or \"check my repo for vulnerabilities\"\n- Running `/security-review` or `/security-review <path>`\n\n## How This Skill Works\n\nUnlike traditional static analysis tools that match patterns, this skill:\n1. **Reads code like a security researcher** — understanding context, intent, and data flow\n2. **Traces across files** — following how user input moves through your application\n3. **Self-verifies findings** — re-examines each result to filter false positives\n4. **Assigns severity ratings** — CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW / INFO\n5. **Proposes targeted patches** — every finding includes a concrete fix\n6. **Requires human approval** — nothing is auto-applied; you always review first\n\n## Execution Workflow\n\nFollow these steps **in order** every time:\n\n### Step 1 — Scope Resolution\nDetermine what to scan:\n- If a path was provided (`/security-review src/auth/`), scan only that scope\n- If no path given, scan the **entire project** starting from the root\n- Identify the language(s) and framework(s) in use (check package.json, requirements.txt,\n  go.mod, Cargo.toml, pom.xml, Gemfile, composer.json, etc.)\n- Read `references/language-patterns.md` to load language-specific vulnerability patterns\n\n### Step 2 — Dependency Audit\nBefore scanning source code, audit dependencies first (fast wins):\n- **Node.js**: Check `package.json` + `package-lock.json` for known vulnerable packages\n- **Python**: Check `requirements.txt` / `pyproject.toml` / `Pipfile`\n- **Java**: Check `pom.xml` / `build.gradle`\n- **Ruby**: Check `Gemfile.lock`\n- **Rust**: Check `Cargo.toml`\n- **Go**: Check `go.sum`\n- Flag packages with known CVEs, deprecated crypto libs, or suspiciously old pinned versions\n- Read `references/vulnerable-packages.md` for a curated watchlist\n\n### Step 3 — Secrets & Exposure Scan\nScan ALL files (including config, env, CI/CD, Dockerfiles, IaC) for:\n- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, passwords, private keys\n- `.env` files accidentally committed\n- Secrets in comments or debug logs\n- Cloud credentials (AWS, GCP, Azure, Stripe, Twilio, etc.)\n- Database connection strings with credentials embedded\n- Read `references/secret-patterns.md` for regex patterns and entropy heuristics to apply\n\n### Step 4 — Vulnerability Deep Scan\nThis is the core scan. Reason about the code — don't just pattern-match.\nRead `references/vuln-categories.md` for full details on each category.\n\n**Injection Flaws**\n- SQL Injection: raw queries with string interpolation, ORM misuse, second-order SQLi\n- XSS: unescaped output, dangerouslySetInnerHTML, innerHTML, template injection\n- Command Injection: exec/spawn/system with user input\n- LDAP, XPath, Header, Log injection\n\n**Authentication & Access Control**\n- Missing authentication on sensitive endpoints\n- Broken object-level authorization (BOLA/IDOR)\n- JWT weaknesses (alg:none, weak secrets, no expiry validation)\n- Session fixation, missing CSRF protection\n- Privilege escalation paths\n- Mass assignment / parameter pollution\n\n**Data Handling**\n- Sensitive data in logs, error messages, or API responses\n- Missing encryption at rest or in transit\n- Insecure deserialization\n- Path traversal / directory traversal\n- XXE (XML External Entity) processing\n- SSRF (Server-Side Request Forgery)\n\n**Cryptography**\n- Use of MD5, SHA1, DES for security purposes\n- Hardcoded IVs or salts\n- Weak random number generation (Math.random() for tokens)\n- Missing TLS certificate validation\n\n**Business Logic**\n- Race conditions (TOCTOU)\n- Integer overflow in financial calculations\n- Missing rate limiting on sensitive endpoints\n- Predictable resource identifiers\n\n### Step 5 — Cross-File Data Flow Analysis\nAfter the per-file scan, perform a **holistic review**:\n- Trace user-controlled input from entry points (HTTP params, headers, body, file uploads)\n  all the way to sinks (DB queries, exec calls, HTML output, file writes)\n- Identify vulnerabilities that only appear when looking at multiple files together\n- Check for insecure trust boundaries between services or modules\n\n### Step 6 — Self-Verification Pass\nFor EACH finding:\n1. Re-read the relevant code with fresh eyes\n2. Ask: \"Is this actually exploitable, or is there sanitization I missed?\"\n3. Check if a framework or middleware already handles this upstream\n4. Downgrade or discard findings that aren't genuine vulnerabilities\n5. Assign final severity: CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW / INFO\n\n### Step 7 — Generate Security Report\nOutput the full report in the format defined in `references/report-format.md`.\n\n### Step 8 — Propose Patches\nFor every CRITICAL and HIGH finding, generate a concrete patch:\n- Show the vulnerable code (before)\n- Show the fixed code (after)\n- Explain what changed and why\n- Preserve the original code style, variable names, and structure\n- Add a comment explaining the fix inline\n\nExplicitly state: **\"Review each patch before applying. Nothing has been changed yet.\"**\n\n## Severity Guide\n\n| Severity | Meaning | Example |\n|----------|---------|---------|\n| 🔴 CRITICAL | Immediate exploitation risk, data breach likely | SQLi, RCE, auth bypass |\n| 🟠 HIGH | Serious vulnerability, exploit path exists | XSS, IDOR, hardcoded secrets |\n| 🟡 MEDIUM | Exploitable with conditions or chaining | CSRF, open redirect, weak crypto |\n| 🔵 LOW | Best practice violation, low direct risk | Verbose errors, missing headers |\n| ⚪ INFO | Observation worth noting, not a vulnerability | Outdated dependency (no CVE) |\n\n## Output Rules\n\n- **Always** produce a findings summary table first (counts by severity)\n- **Never** auto-apply any patch — present patches for human review only\n- **Always** include a confidence rating per finding (High / Medium / Low)\n- **Group findings** by category, not by file\n- **Be specific** — include file path, line number, and the exact vulnerable code snippet\n- **Explain the risk** in plain English — what could an attacker do with this?\n- If the codebase is clean, say so clearly: \"No vulnerabilities found\" with what was scanned\n\n## Reference Files\n\nFor detailed detection guidance, load the following reference files as needed:\n\n- `references/vuln-categories.md` — Deep reference for every vulnerability category with detection signals, safe patterns, and escalation checkers\n  - Search patterns: `SQL injection`, `XSS`, `command injection`, `SSRF`, `BOLA`, `IDOR`, `JWT`, `CSRF`, `secrets`, `cryptography`, `race condition`, `path traversal`\n- `references/secret-patterns.md` — Regex patterns, entropy-based detection, and CI/CD secret risks\n  - Search patterns: `API key`, `token`, `private key`, `connection string`, `entropy`, `.env`, `GitHub Actions`, `Docker`, `Terraform`\n- `references/language-patterns.md` — Framework-specific vulnerability patterns for JavaScript, Python, Java, PHP, Go, Ruby, and Rust\n  - Search patterns: `Express`, `React`, `Next.js`, `Django`, `Flask`, `FastAPI`, `Spring Boot`, `PHP`, `Go`, `Rails`, `Rust`\n- `references/vulnerable-packages.md` — Curated CVE watchlist for npm, pip, Maven, Rubygems, Cargo, and Go modules\n  - Search patterns: `lodash`, `axios`, `jsonwebtoken`, `Pillow`, `log4j`, `nokogiri`, `CVE`\n- `references/report-format.md` — Structured output template for security reports with finding cards, dependency audit, secrets scan, and patch proposal formatting\n  - detection guidance for every vulnerability category.\nLoad this during Step 4 of the scan workflow.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Injection Flaws\n\n### SQL Injection\n**What to look for:**\n- String concatenation or interpolation inside SQL queries\n- Raw `.query()`, `.execute()`, `.raw()` calls with variables\n- ORM `whereRaw()`, `selectRaw()`, `orderByRaw()` with user input\n- Second-order SQLi: data stored safely, then used unsafely later\n- Stored procedures called with unsanitized input\n\n**Detection signals (all languages):**\n```\n\"SELECT ... \" + variable\n`SELECT ... ${variable}`\nf\"SELECT ... {variable}\"\n\"SELECT ... %s\" % variable   # Only safe with proper driver parameterization\ncursor.execute(\"... \" + input)\ndb.raw(`... ${req.params.id}`)\n```\n\n**Safe patterns (parameterized):**\n```js\ndb.query('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?', [userId])\nUser.findOne({ where: { id: userId } })  // ORM safe\n```\n\n**Escalation checkers:**\n- Is the query result ever used in another query? (second-order)\n- Is the table/column name user-controlled? (cannot be parameterized — must allowlist)\n\n---\n\n### Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)\n**What to look for:**\n- `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `document.write()` with user data\n- `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in React\n- Template engines rendering unescaped: `{{{ var }}}` (Handlebars), `!= var` (Pug)\n- jQuery `.html()`, `.append()` with user data\n- `eval()`, `setTimeout(string)`, `setInterval(string)` with user data\n- DOM-based: `location.hash`, `document.referrer`, `window.name` written to DOM\n- Stored XSS: user input saved to DB, rendered without escaping later\n\n**Detection by framework:**\n- **React**: Safe by default EXCEPT `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`\n- **Angular**: Safe by default EXCEPT `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`\n- **Vue**: Safe by default EXCEPT `v-html`\n- **Vanilla JS**: Every DOM write is suspect\n\n---\n\n### Command Injection\n**What to look for (Node.js):**\n```js\nexec(userInput)\nexecSync(`ping ${host}`)\nspawn('sh', ['-c', userInput])\nchild_process.exec('ls ' + dir)\n```\n\n**What to look for (Python):**\n```python\nos.system(user_input)\nsubprocess.call(user_input, shell=True)\neval(user_input)\n```\n\n**What to look for (PHP):**\n```php\nexec($input)\nsystem($_GET['cmd'])\npassthru($input)\n`$input`  # backtick operator\n```\n\n**Safe alternatives:** Use array form of spawn/subprocess without shell=True; use allowlists for commands.\n\n---\n\n### Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)\n**What to look for:**\n- HTTP requests where the URL is user-controlled\n- Webhooks, URL preview, image fetch features\n- PDF generators that fetch external URLs\n- Redirects to user-supplied URLs\n\n**High-risk targets:**\n- AWS metadata service: `169.254.169.254`\n- Internal services: `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `10.x.x.x`, `192.168.x.x`\n- Cloud metadata endpoints\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\nfetch(req.body.url)\naxios.get(userSuppliedUrl)\nhttp.get(params.webhook)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Authentication & Access Control\n\n### Broken Object Level Authorization (BOLA / IDOR)\n**What to look for:**\n- Resource IDs taken directly from URL/params without ownership check\n- `findById(req.params.id)` without verifying `userId === currentUser.id`\n- Numeric sequential IDs (easily guessable)\n\n**Example vulnerable pattern:**\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: no ownership check\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findById(req.params.id);\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n\n// SAFE: verify ownership\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findOne({ _id: req.params.id, owner: req.user.id });\n  if (!doc) return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Forbidden' });\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n### JWT Vulnerabilities\n**What to look for:**\n- `alg: \"none\"` accepted\n- Weak or hardcoded secrets: `secret`, `password`, `1234`\n- No expiry (`exp` claim) validation\n- Algorithm confusion (RS256 → HS256 downgrade)\n- JWT stored in `localStorage` (XSS risk; prefer httpOnly cookie)\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\njwt.verify(token, secret, { algorithms: ['HS256'] })  // Check algorithms array\njwt.decode(token)  // WARNING: decode does NOT verify signature\n```\n\n---\n\n### Missing Authentication / Authorization\n**What to look for:**\n- Admin or sensitive endpoints missing auth middleware\n- Routes defined after `app.use(authMiddleware)` vs before it\n- Feature flags or debug endpoints left exposed in production\n- GraphQL resolvers missing auth checks at field level\n\n---\n\n### CSRF\n**What to look for:**\n- State-changing operations (POST/PUT/DELETE) without CSRF token\n- APIs relying only on cookies for auth without SameSite attribute\n- Missing `SameSite=Strict` or `SameSite=Lax` on session cookies\n\n---\n\n## 3. Secrets & Sensitive Data Exposure\n\n### In-Code Secrets\nLook for patterns like:\n```\nAPI_KEY=[REDACTED]\npassword=[REDACTED]\nSECRET=[REDACTED]\nprivate_key = \"-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\"\naws_secret_access_key = \"wJalrXUtn...\"\n```\n\nEntropy heuristic: strings > 20 chars with high character variety in assignment context\nare likely secrets even if the variable name doesn't say so.\n\n### In Logs / Error Messages\n```js\nconsole.log('User password:[REDACTED], password)\nlogger.info({ user, token })   // token shouldn't be logged\nres.status(500).json({ error: err.stack })  // stack traces expose internals\n```\n\n### Sensitive Data in API Responses\n- Returning full user object including `password_hash`, `ssn`, `credit_card`\n- Including internal IDs or system paths in error responses\n\n---\n\n## 4. Cryptography\n\n### Weak Algorithms\n| Algorithm | Issue | Replace With |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| MD5 | Broken for security | SHA-256 or bcrypt (passwords) |\n| SHA-1 | Collision attacks | SHA-256 |\n| DES / 3DES | Weak key size | AES-256-GCM |\n| RC4 | Broken | AES-GCM |\n| ECB mode | No IV, patterns visible | GCM or CBC with random IV |\n\n### Weak Randomness\n```js\n// VULNERABLE\nMath.random()                    // not cryptographically secure\nDate.now()                       // predictable\nMath.random().toString(36)       // weak token generation\n\n// SAFE\ncrypto.randomBytes(32)           // Node.js\nsecrets.token_urlsafe(32)        // Python\n```\n\n### Password Hashing\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nhashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()\nhashlib.sha256(password.encode()).hexdigest()\n\n# SAFE\nbcrypt.hashpw(password, bcrypt.gensalt(rounds=12))\nargon2.hash(password)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Insecure Dependencies\n\n### What to flag:\n- Packages with known CVEs in installed version range\n- Packages abandoned > 2 years with no security updates\n- Packages with extremely broad permissions for their stated purpose\n- Transitive dependencies pulling in known-bad packages\n- Pinned versions that are significantly behind current (possible unpatched vulns)\n\n### High-risk package watchlist: see `references/vulnerable-packages.md`\n\n---\n\n## 6. Business Logic\n\n### Race Conditions (TOCTOU)\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: check then act without atomic lock\nconst balance = await getBalance(userId);\nif (balance >= amount) {\n  await deductBalance(userId, amount);  // race condition between check and deduct\n}\n\n// SAFE: use atomic DB transaction or optimistic locking\nawait db.transaction(async (trx) => {\n  const user = await User.query(trx).forUpdate().findById(userId);\n  if (user.balance < amount) throw new Error('Insufficient funds');\n  await user.$query(trx).patch({ balance: user.balance - amount });\n});\n```\n\n### Missing Rate Limiting\nFlag endpoints that:\n- Accept authentication credentials (login, 2FA)\n- Send emails or SMS\n- Perform expensive operations\n- Expose user enumeration (password reset, registration)\n\n---\n\n## 7. Path Traversal\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nfilename = request.args.get('file')\nwith open(f'/var/uploads/{filename}') as f:  # ../../../../etc/passwd\n\n# SAFE\nfilename = os.path.basename(request.args.get('file'))\nsafe_path = os.path.join('/var/uploads', filename)\nif not safe_path.startswith('/var/uploads/'):\n    abort(400)\n```\n\n---\n[Tool result call_lvsVr8rsz26rKjCKfSm2iLWE]\n# Vulnerable & High-Risk Package Watchlist\n\nLoad this during Step 2 (Dependency Audit). 3.4.0 | Authentication bypass | >= 3.4.0 |\n| requests | < 2.31.0 | Proxy auth info leak | >= 2.31.0 |\n| urllib3 | < 2.0.7 | Header injection | >= 2.0.7 |\n| Django | < 4.2.16 | Various | >= 4.2.16       "content": "[Tool result call_LYtDRMn6rI9j2V5T08mKh0zk]\nname: release\r\n\r\non:\r\n  push:\r\n    tags:\r\n      - \"v*\"\r\n\r\npermissions:\r\n  contents: write    # create GH Release + upload assets\r\n  id-token:[REDACTED]    # npm provenance\r\n\r\njobs:\r\n  build:\r\n    # macOS runner: bun cross-compiles all four targets from darwin-arm64,\r\n    # and darwin outputs get ad-hoc linker-signed so macOS will actually exec\r\n    # them. Cross-compiling darwin targets from linux produces unsigned\r\n    # Mach-O binaries that macOS SIGKILLs on first run.\r\n    runs-on: macos-latest\r\n    outputs:\r\n      version: ${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout (no submodules — skvm-data not needed for build)\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Setup Bun\r\n        uses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v2\r\n        with:\r\n          # Pinned: bun 1.3.12 on macos-arm64 stopped ad-hoc linker-signing\r\n          # `--compile --target=bun-darwin-*` outputs, producing binaries that\r\n          # macOS SIGKILLs. 1.3.11 still signs them correctly. Revisit when\r\n          # upstream bun ships a fix.\r\n          bun-version: \"1.3.11\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Install deps (skip postinstall — dev repo guard already handles it, but be explicit)\r\n        run: SKVM_SKIP_POSTINSTALL=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile\r\n\r\n      - name: Typecheck\r\n        run: bun run typecheck\r\n\r\n      - name: Resolve version from tag\r\n        id: version\r\n        run: |\r\n          tag=\"${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/}\"\r\n          version=\"${tag#v}\"\r\n          echo \"tag=${tag}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\r\n          echo \"version=${version}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Sync package.json version to tag\r\n        run: |\r\n          node -e \"\r\n            const fs = require('fs');\r\n            const p = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('package.json','utf8'));\r\n            p.version = '${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}';\r\n            fs.writeFileSync('package.json', JSON.stringify(p, null, 2) + '\\n');\r\n          \"\r\n\r\n      - name: Build all targets (bun cross-compilation)\r\n        run: bun run build:all\r\n\r\n      - name: Upload build artifacts\r\n        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          name: release-tarballs\r\n          path: |\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz.sha256\r\n          retention-days: 7\r\n\r\n  release:\r\n    needs: build\r\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Download build artifacts\r\n        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          name: release-tarballs\r\n          path: dist/\r\n\r\n      - name: Create GitHub Release\r\n        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2\r\n        with:\r\n          draft: false\r\n          prerelease: false\r\n          generate_release_notes: true\r\n          files: |\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz.sha256\r\n\r\n  npm-publish:\r\n    needs: [build, release]\r\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\r\n    permissions:\r\n      contents: read\r\n      id-token:[REDACTED]\r\n    # Kill switch: publishing is enabled by default; set the\r\n    # ENABLE_NPM_PUBLISH repo variable to \"false\" to skip this job\r\n    # (e.g. during staged rollouts or if npm is broken).\r\n    if: ${{ vars.ENABLE_NPM_PUBLISH != 'false' }}\r\n    # Trusted Publishing: auth is via GitHub Actions OIDC JWT, not NPM_TOKEN.\r\n    # The `id-token:[REDACTED] permission at the top of this workflow issues the\r\n    # JWT; the npm side must have this workflow configured as a Trusted\r\n    # Publisher under @ipads-skvm/skvm's package settings. No secrets here.\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Setup Node\r\n        uses: actions/setup-node@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          # npm Trusted Publishing currently requires npm >= 11.5.1\r\n          # and Node >= 22.14.0; use a current LTS/runtime here.\r\n          node-version: \"24\"\r\n          registry-url: \"https://registry.npmjs.org\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Sync package.json version to tag\r\n        run: |\r\n          version=\"${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/v}\"\r\n          node -e \"\r\n            const fs = require('fs');\r\n            const p = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('package.json','utf8'));\r\n            p.version = '${version}';\r\n            fs.writeFileSync('package.json', JSON.stringify(p, null, 2) + '\\n');\r\n          \"\r\n\r\n      - name: Publish to npm\r\n        run: npm publish --access public\r\n"

It is directly under `source/`, not under a conventional `.github/workflows/` directory, but it was treated as the supplied workflow artifact.\\n\\nThe workflow triggers on pushed tags matching `v*` (`source/release.yml:3-6`). Its workflow-level permission map declares:\\n\\n```yaml\\npermissions:\\n  contents: write\\n  id-token:[REDACTED] `source/release.yml:8-10`.\\n\\nPermission declaration states were kept distinct:\\n\\n| Job | Declaration state | Statically effective permissions | Evidence |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| `build` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:13-21`, inherited from `:8-10` |\\n| `release` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:71-74`, inherited from `:8-10` |\\n| `npm-publish` | **Explicit map** | `contents: read`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:99-101` |\\n\\nNo workflow or job uses an **explicitly empty** permission declaration such as `permissions: {}`. Omitted job permissions are not recorded as explicitly empty: `build` and `release` inherit the workflow-level map.\\n\\nEight `uses:` occurrences were found, comprising six unique references. Every reference uses a mutable major-version tag rather than a full commit SHA:\\n\\n- `actions/checkout@v4` — lines 23, 76, 112\\n- `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` — line 28\\n- `actions/upload-artifact@v4` — line 63\\n- `actions/download-artifact@v4` — line 81\\n- `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` — line 87\\n- `actions/setup-node@v4` — line 117\\n\\n## Findings by Category\\n\\n### Authentication and Access Control: Workflow Token Permissions\\n\\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  \\n🟠 **HIGH — Build and release jobs inherit broader token authority than each job requires**  \\n**Confidence: HIGH**  \\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\\n\\n**Locations:**\\n\\n- Workflow permission map: `source/release.yml:8-10`\\n- `build` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:13-21`\\n- Dependency installation and build commands: `source/release.yml:36-60`\\n- `release` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:71-74`\\n- GitHub release operation: `source/release.yml:86-94`\\n- Narrower `npm-publish` job map: `source/release.yml:99-101`\\n\\n**Relevant code:**\\n\\n```yaml\\npermissions:\\n  contents: write\\n  id-token:[REDACTED]  build:\\n    runs-on: macos-latest\\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\\n\\n  release:\\n    needs: build\\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\\n```\\n\\n**Risk:**\\n\\nThe `build` job inherits both `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED] while checking out code, invoking a third-party setup action, installing dependencies, and running project build commands. If any executed action, dependency lifecycle path, or build script were compromised, the job would have repository-content write authority and could request an OIDC token. The build steps shown do not demonstrate a need for either capability beyond ordinary source checkout.\\n\\nThe `release` job needs `contents: write` to create the GitHub release, but it also inherits `id-token:[REDACTED] even though no release step shown requests OIDC authentication. Granting OIDC token issuance to jobs that do not use it unnecessarily expands the credential surface.\\n\\nThe `npm-publish` job is better scoped: it explicitly declares `contents: read` and `id-token:[REDACTED] at lines 99-101.\\n\\n**Recommended fix (proposal only):**\\n\\nDeclare least-privilege permissions per job instead of granting both write capabilities at workflow scope. Based only on the visible steps, a likely structure is:\\n\\n```yaml\\npermissions: {}\\n\\njobs:\\n  build:\\n    permissions:\\n      contents: read\\n\\n  release:\\n    permissions:\\n      contents: write\\n\\n  npm-publish:\\n    permissions:\\n      contents: read\\n      id-token:[REDACTED] whether artifact upload/download behavior or repository policy requires any additional scope before adopting this proposal.\\n\\n**Reference:** GitHub Actions least-privilege `GITHUB_TOKEN` and OIDC permission design.\\n\\n**Self-verification:**\\n\\n- Re-read all three job declarations and the workflow-level map.\\n- Confirmed `build` has no job-level `permissions` key between lines 13-70.\\n- Confirmed `release` has no job-level `permissions` key between lines 71-95.\\n- Confirmed `npm-publish` has an explicit override at lines 99-101.\\n- Confirmed no visible `build` or `release` step explicitly consumes an OIDC token.\\n- No upstream reusable workflow or composite action can change the fact that these jobs receive the declared token permissions; however, offline review cannot observe repository rules or runtime token use inside fetched actions.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Supply-Chain Integrity: Action References\\n\\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  \\n🟠 **HIGH — All external actions are referenced by mutable major-version tags**  \\n**Confidence: HIGH**  \\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\\n\\n**Locations:**\\n\\n- `actions/checkout@v4` — `source/release.yml:23`, `:76`, `:112`\\n- `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` — `source/release.yml:28`\\n- `actions/upload-artifact@v4` — `source/release.yml:63`\\n- `actions/download-artifact@v4` — `source/release.yml:81`\\n- `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` — `source/release.yml:87`\\n- `actions/setup-node@v4` — `source/release.yml:117`\\n\\n**Representative code:**\\n\\n```yaml\\nuses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v2\\nuses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2\\nuses: actions/setup-node@v4\\n```\\n\\n**Risk:**\\n\\nMajor-version tags are movable references. A future tag update or upstream compromise can change the code executed by this release workflow without a corresponding commit in this repository. The impact is elevated because:\\n\\n- `build` executes tagged actions while holding inherited `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED] `release` executes tagged actions while holding inherited `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED] `npm-publish` executes tagged actions while able to request an OIDC token used for trusted npm publishing.\\n\\nA compromised action in these jobs could misuse the job's token, alter release inputs, tamper with artifacts available to later steps, or interfere with package publication.\\n\\n**Recommended fix (proposal only):**\\n\\nPin every external action to a reviewed full-length commit SHA, retaining the release tag in a comment for maintainability. Example form:\\n\\n```yaml\\n- uses: actions/checkout@<reviewed-40-character-commit-sha> # v4\\n```\\n\\nUse a controlled update mechanism to review and advance pinned SHAs. Exact SHAs are intentionally not proposed here because this was an offline review and no upstream reference resolution or authenticity verification was performed.\\n\\n**Reference:** GitHub Actions supply-chain hardening; immutable action pinning.\\n\\n**Self-verification:**\\n\\n- Enumerated every `uses:` key in the supplied file; there are eight occurrences and six unique references.\\n- Confirmed each suffix is `@v2` or `@v4`, not a full commit SHA.\\n- No local actions (`./path`) or reusable-workflow `uses:` references were present.\\n- Did not infer that any referenced version is currently compromised or vulnerable; the finding concerns mutable reference integrity only.\\n\\n## Patch Proposals\\n\\n**Review each patch before applying. Nothing has been changed yet.**\\n\\nNo source patch was applied, as requested. The snippets above are design proposals only. Exact action SHAs require separate, trusted upstream resolution and review.\\n\\n## Self-Verification Summary\\n\\nA second static pass was performed against `source/release.yml` to verify:\\n\\n1. Workflow-level permissions and line evidence.\\n2. Whether each job declares, omits, or explicitly empties permissions.\\n3. Effective static inheritance for jobs with omitted declarations.\\n4. Every `uses:` occurrence and its exact reference suffix.\\n5. Whether a full commit SHA was used anywhere.\\n6. Whether findings were limited to permission and action-reference concerns.\\n\\nThe pass confirmed that `build` and `release` omit job-level permissions and inherit the workflow map; `npm-publish` explicitly overrides it; no permission map is explicitly empty; and all eight action uses are tag-based.\\n\\n## Remaining Review Limits\\n\\n- This was an **offline static review**. No workflows, actions, scripts, package managers, or deployment operations were executed.\\n- No live GitHub, marketplace, advisory, npm, repository-setting, environment, branch-protection, tag-protection, or deployment state was queried.\\n- No claim is made about live CVEs, current action-tag targets, upstream action integrity, action ownership, or whether deployed releases/packages match this file.\\n- Exact commit SHAs behind action tags were not resolved or verified.\\n- Repository/organization default token settings, rulesets, protected environments, trusted-publisher configuration, and who may create matching tags were not available for verification.\\n- The contents of remote actions referenced by `uses:` were not available offline and were not audited.\\n- Project scripts invoked by `run:` steps and dependency lifecycle behavior were not reviewed because unrelated whole-repository audit duties are outside this task's scope.\\n- The supplied file's location is nonstandard for a checked-in GitHub workflow. OAuth\n[0-9]+-[0-9A-Za-z_]{32}\\.apps\\.googleusercontent\\.com\n\n# Cloudflare (near CF_API_TOKEN)\n[a-zA-Z0-9_\\-]{37}\n\n# Mailgun\nkey-[a-zA-Z0-9]{32}\n\n# CERTIFICATE-----\n```\n\n### Database Connection Strings\n```regex\n# MongoDB\nmongodb(\\+srv)?:\\/\\/[^:]+:[^@]+@\n\n# PostgreSQL / MySQL\n(postgres|postgresql|mysql):\\/\\/[^:]+:[^@]+@\n\n# Redis Entropy-Based Detection\n\nApply to string literals > 20 characters in assignment context.\nHigh entropy (Shannon entropy > 4.5 bits/char) + length > 20 = likely secret.\n\n```\nCalculate entropy: -sum(p * log2(p)) for each character frequency p\nThreshold: > 4.5 bits/char AND > 20 chars AND assigned to a variable\n```\n\nCommon false positives to exclude:\n- Lorem ipsum text\n- HTML/CSS content\n- Base64-encoded non-sensitive config (but flag and note)\n- UUID/GUID (entropy is high but format is recognizable)\n\n---\n\n## Files That Should Never Be Committed\n\nFlag if these files       "output": "# Language-Specific Vulnerability Patterns\n\nLoad the relevant section during Step 1 (Scope Resolution) after identifying languages.\n\n---\n\n## JavaScript / TypeScript (Node.js, React, Next.js, Express)\n\n### Critical APIs/calls to flag\n```js\neval()                    // arbitrary code execution\nFunction('return ...')   // same as eval\nchild_process.exec()     // command injection if user input reaches it\nfs.readFile              // path traversal if user controls path\nfs.writeFile             // path traversal if user controls path\n```\n\n### Express.js specific\n```js\n// Missing helmet (security headers)\nconst app = express()\n// Should have: app.use(helmet())\n\n// Body size limits missing (DoS)\napp.use(express.json())\n// Should have: app.use(express.json({ limit: '10kb' }))\n\n// CORS misconfiguration\napp.use(cors({ origin: '*' }))  // too permissive\napp.use(cors({ origin: req.headers.origin }))  // reflects any origin\n\n// Trust proxy without validation\napp.set('trust proxy', true)  // only safe behind known proxy\n```\n\n### React specific\n```jsx\n<div dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: userContent }} />  // XSS\n<a href={userUrl}>link</a>  // javascript: URL injection\n```\n\n### Next.js specific\n```js\n// Server Actions without auth\nexport async function deleteUser(id) {   // missing: auth check\n  await db.users.delete(id)\n}\n\n// API Routes missing method validation\nexport default function handler(req, res) {\n  // Should check: if (req.method !== 'POST') return res.status(405)\n  doSensitiveAction()\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Python (Django, Flask, FastAPI)\n\n### Django specific\n```python\n# Raw SQL\nUser.objects.raw(f\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = '{name}'\")  # SQLi\n\n# Missing CSRF\n@csrf_exempt  # Only OK for APIs with token auth\n\n# Debug mode in production\nDEBUG = True  # in settings.py — exposes stack traces\n\n# SECRET_KEY\nSECRET_KEY = 'django-insecure-...'  # must be changed for production\n\n# ALLOWED_HOSTS\nALLOWED_HOSTS = ['*']  # too permissive\n```\n\n### Flask specific\n```python\n# Debug mode\napp.run(debug=True)  # never in production\n\n# Secret key\napp.secret_key = 'dev'  # weak\n\n# eval/exec with user input\neval(request.args.get('expr'))\n\n# render_template_string with user input (SSTI)\nrender_template_string(f\"Hello {name}\")  # Server-Side Template Injection\n```\n\n### FastAPI specific\n```python\n# Missing auth dependency\n@app.delete(\"/users/{user_id}\")  # No Depends(get_current_user)\nasync def delete_user(user_id: int):\n    ...\n\n# Arbitrary file read\n@app.get(\"/files/{filename}\")\nasync def read_file(filename: str):\n    return FileResponse(f\"uploads/{filename}\")  # path traversal\n```\n\n---\n\n## Java (Spring Boot)\n\n### Spring Boot specific\n```java\n// SQL Injection\nString query = \"SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = '\" + name + \"'\";\njdbcTemplate.query(query, ...);\n\n// XXE\nDocumentBuilderFactory dbf = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance();\n// Missing: dbf.setFeature(\"http://apache.org/xml/features/disallow-doctype-decl\", true)\n\n// Deserialization\nObjectInputStream ois = new ObjectInputStream(inputStream);\nObject obj = ois.readObject();  // only safe with allowlist\n\n// Spring Security — permitAll on sensitive endpoint\n.antMatchers(\"/admin/**\").permitAll()\n\n// Actuator endpoints exposed\nmanagement.endpoints.web.exposure.include=*  # in application.properties\n```\n\n---\n\n## PHP\n\n```php\n// Direct user input in queries\n$result = mysql_query(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = \" . $_GET['id']);\n\n// File inclusion\ninclude($_GET['page'] . \".php\");  // local/remote file inclusion\n\n// eval\neval($_POST['code']);\n\n// extract() with user input\nextract($_POST);  // overwrites any variable\n\n// Loose comparison\nif ($password=[REDACTED] \"admin\") {}  // use === instead\n\n// Unserialize\nunserialize($_COOKIE['data']);  // remote code execution\n```\n\n---\n\n## Go\n\n```go\n// Command injection\nexec.Command(\"sh\", \"-c\", userInput)\n\n// SQL injection\ndb.Query(\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE name = '\" + name + \"'\")\n\n// Path traversal\nfilePath := filepath.Join(\"/uploads/\", userInput)  // sanitize userInput first\n\n// Insecure TLS\nhttp.Transport{TLSClientConfig: &tls.Config{InsecureSkipVerify: true}}\n\n// Goroutine leak / missing context cancellation\ngo func() {\n  // No done channel or context\n  for { ... }\n}()\n```\n\n---\n\n## Ruby on Rails\n\n```ruby\n# SQL injection (safe alternatives use placeholders)\nUser.where(\"name = '#{params[:name]}'\")  # VULNERABLE\nUser.where(\"name = ?\", params[:name])   # SAFE\n\n# Mass assignment without strong params\n@user.update(params[:user])  # should be params.require(:user).permit(...)\n\n# eval / send with user input\neval(params[:code])\nsend(params[:method])  # arbitrary method call\n\n# Redirect to user-supplied URL (open redirect)\nredirect_to params[:url]\n\n# YAML.load (allows arbitrary object creation)\nYAML.load(user_input)  # use YAML.safe_load instead\n```\n\n---\n\n## Rust\n\n```rust\n// Unsafe blocks — flag for manual review\nunsafe {\n    // Reason for unsafety should be documented\n}\n\n// Integer overflow (debug builds panic, release silently wraps)\nlet result = a + b;  // use checked_add/saturating_add for financial math\n\n// Unwrap/expect in production code (panics on None/Err)\nlet value = option.unwrap();  // prefer ? or match\n\n// Deserializing arbitrary types\nserde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&user_input)  // generally safe\n// But: bincode::deserialize from untrusted input — can be exploited\n```\n",

      "output": "# Vulnerability Categories — Deep Reference\n\nThis file contains detailed detection guidance for every vulnerability category.\nLoad this during Step 4 of the scan workflow.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Injection Flaws\n\n### SQL Injection\n**What to look for:**\n- String concatenation or interpolation inside SQL queries\n- Raw `.query()`, `.execute()`, `.raw()` calls with variables\n- ORM `whereRaw()`, `selectRaw()`, `orderByRaw()` with user input\n- Second-order SQLi: data stored safely, then used unsafely later\n- Stored procedures called with unsanitized input\n\n**Detection signals (all languages):**\n```\n\"SELECT ... \" + variable\n`SELECT ... ${variable}`\nf\"SELECT ... {variable}\"\n\"SELECT ... %s\" % variable   # Only safe with proper driver parameterization\ncursor.execute(\"... \" + input)\ndb.raw(`... ${req.params.id}`)\n```\n\n**Safe patterns (parameterized):**\n```js\ndb.query('SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ?', [userId])\nUser.findOne({ where: { id: userId } })  // ORM safe\n```\n\n**Escalation checkers:**\n- Is the query result ever used in another query? (second-order)\n- Is the table/column name user-controlled? (cannot be parameterized — must allowlist)\n\n---\n\n### Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)\n**What to look for:**\n- `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `document.write()` with user data\n- `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` in React\n- Template engines rendering unescaped: `{{{ var }}}` (Handlebars), `!= var` (Pug)\n- jQuery `.html()`, `.append()` with user data\n- `eval()`, `setTimeout(string)`, `setInterval(string)` with user data\n- DOM-based: `location.hash`, `document.referrer`, `window.name` written to DOM\n- Stored XSS: user input saved to DB, rendered without escaping later\n\n**Detection by framework:**\n- **React**: Safe by default EXCEPT `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`\n- **Angular**: Safe by default EXCEPT `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`\n- **Vue**: Safe by default EXCEPT `v-html`\n- **Vanilla JS**: Every DOM write is suspect\n\n---\n\n### Command Injection\n**What to look for (Node.js):**\n```js\nexec(userInput)\nexecSync(`ping ${host}`)\nspawn('sh', ['-c', userInput])\nchild_process.exec('ls ' + dir)\n```\n\n**What to look for (Python):**\n```python\nos.system(user_input)\nsubprocess.call(user_input, shell=True)\neval(user_input)\n```\n\n**What to look for (PHP):**\n```php\nexec($input)\nsystem($_GET['cmd'])\npassthru($input)\n`$input`  # backtick operator\n```\n\n**Safe alternatives:** Use array form of spawn/subprocess without shell=True; use allowlists for commands.\n\n---\n\n### Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)\n**What to look for:**\n- HTTP requests where the URL is user-controlled\n- Webhooks, URL preview, image fetch features\n- PDF generators that fetch external URLs\n- Redirects to user-supplied URLs\n\n**High-risk targets:**\n- AWS metadata service: `169.254.169.254`\n- Internal services: `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `10.x.x.x`, `192.168.x.x`\n- Cloud metadata endpoints\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\nfetch(req.body.url)\naxios.get(userSuppliedUrl)\nhttp.get(params.webhook)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Authentication & Access Control\n\n### Broken Object Level Authorization (BOLA / IDOR)\n**What to look for:**\n- Resource IDs taken directly from URL/params without ownership check\n- `findById(req.params.id)` without verifying `userId === currentUser.id`\n- Numeric sequential IDs (easily guessable)\n\n**Example vulnerable pattern:**\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: no ownership check\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findById(req.params.id);\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n\n// SAFE: verify ownership\napp.get('/api/documents/:id', async (req, res) => {\n  const doc = await Document.findOne({ _id: req.params.id, owner: req.user.id });\n  if (!doc) return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Forbidden' });\n  res.json(doc);\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n### JWT Vulnerabilities\n**What to look for:**\n- `alg: \"none\"` accepted\n- Weak or hardcoded secrets: `secret`, `password`, `1234`\n- No expiry (`exp` claim) validation\n- Algorithm confusion (RS256 → HS256 downgrade)\n- JWT stored in `localStorage` (XSS risk; prefer httpOnly cookie)\n\n**Detection:**\n```js\njwt.verify(token, secret, { algorithms: ['HS256'] })  // Check algorithms array\njwt.decode(token)  // WARNING: decode does NOT verify signature\n```\n\n---\n\n### Missing Authentication / Authorization\n**What to look for:**\n- Admin or sensitive endpoints missing auth middleware\n- Routes defined after `app.use(authMiddleware)` vs before it\n- Feature flags or debug endpoints left exposed in production\n- GraphQL resolvers missing auth checks at field level\n\n---\n\n### CSRF\n**What to look for:**\n- State-changing operations (POST/PUT/DELETE) without CSRF token\n- APIs relying only on cookies for auth without SameSite attribute\n- Missing `SameSite=Strict` or `SameSite=Lax` on session cookies\n\n---\n\n## 3. Secrets & Sensitive Data Exposure\n\n### In-Code Secrets\nLook for patterns like:\n```\nAPI_KEY=[REDACTED]\npassword=[REDACTED]\nSECRET=[REDACTED]\nprivate_key = \"-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\"\naws_secret_access_key = \"wJalrXUtn...\"\n```\n\nEntropy heuristic: strings > 20 chars with high character variety in assignment context\nare likely secrets even if the variable name doesn't say so.\n\n### In Logs / Error Messages\n```js\nconsole.log('User password:[REDACTED], password)\nlogger.info({ user, token })   // token shouldn't be logged\nres.status(500).json({ error: err.stack })  // stack traces expose internals\n```\n\n### Sensitive Data in API Responses\n- Returning full user object including `password_hash`, `ssn`, `credit_card`\n- Including internal IDs or system paths in error responses\n\n---\n\n## 4. Cryptography\n\n### Weak Algorithms\n| Algorithm | Issue | Replace With |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| MD5 | Broken for security | SHA-256 or bcrypt (passwords) |\n| SHA-1 | Collision attacks | SHA-256 |\n| DES / 3DES | Weak key size | AES-256-GCM |\n| RC4 | Broken | AES-GCM |\n| ECB mode | No IV, patterns visible | GCM or CBC with random IV |\n\n### Weak Randomness\n```js\n// VULNERABLE\nMath.random()                    // not cryptographically secure\nDate.now()                       // predictable\nMath.random().toString(36)       // weak token generation\n\n// SAFE\ncrypto.randomBytes(32)           // Node.js\nsecrets.token_urlsafe(32)        // Python\n```\n\n### Password Hashing\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nhashlib.md5(password.encode()).hexdigest()\nhashlib.sha256(password.encode()).hexdigest()\n\n# SAFE\nbcrypt.hashpw(password, bcrypt.gensalt(rounds=12))\nargon2.hash(password)\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Insecure Dependencies\n\n### What to flag:\n- Packages with known CVEs in installed version range\n- Packages abandoned > 2 years with no security updates\n- Packages with extremely broad permissions for their stated purpose\n- Transitive dependencies pulling in known-bad packages\n- Pinned versions that are significantly behind current (possible unpatched vulns)\n\n### High-risk package watchlist: see `references/vulnerable-packages.md`\n\n---\n\n## 6. Business Logic\n\n### Race Conditions (TOCTOU)\n```js\n// VULNERABLE: check then act without atomic lock\nconst balance = await getBalance(userId);\nif (balance >= amount) {\n  await deductBalance(userId, amount);  // race condition between check and deduct\n}\n\n// SAFE: use atomic DB transaction or optimistic locking\nawait db.transaction(async (trx) => {\n  const user = await User.query(trx).forUpdate().findById(userId);\n  if (user.balance < amount) throw new Error('Insufficient funds');\n  await user.$query(trx).patch({ balance: user.balance - amount });\n});\n```\n\n### Missing Rate Limiting\nFlag endpoints that:\n- Accept authentication credentials (login, 2FA)\n- Send emails or SMS\n- Perform expensive operations\n- Expose user enumeration (password reset, registration)\n\n---\n\n## 7. Path Traversal\n```python\n# VULNERABLE\nfilename = request.args.get('file')\nwith open(f'/var/uploads/{filename}') as f:  # ../../../../etc/passwd\n\n# SAFE\nfilename = os.path.basename(request.args.get('file'))\nsafe_path = os.path.join('/var/uploads', filename)\nif not safe_path.startswith('/var/uploads/'):\n    abort(400)\n```\n",

Cross-compiling darwin targets from linux produces unsigned\r\n    # Mach-O binaries that macOS SIGKILLs on first run.\r\n    runs-on: macos-latest\r\n    outputs:\r\n      version: ${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout (no submodules — skvm-data not needed for build)\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Setup Bun\r\n        uses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v2\r\n        with:\r\n          # Pinned: bun 1.3.12 on macos-arm64 stopped ad-hoc linker-signing\r\n          # `--compile --target=bun-darwin-*` outputs, producing binaries that\r\n          # macOS SIGKILLs. 1.3.11 still signs them correctly. Revisit when\r\n          # upstream bun ships a fix.\r\n          bun-version: \"1.3.11\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Install deps (skip postinstall — dev repo guard already handles it, but be explicit)\r\n        run: SKVM_SKIP_POSTINSTALL=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile\r\n\r\n      - name: Typecheck\r\n        run: bun run typecheck\r\n\r\n      - name: Resolve version from tag\r\n        id: version\r\n        run: |\r\n          tag=\"${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/}\"\r\n          version=\"${tag#v}\"\r\n          echo \"tag=${tag}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\r\n          echo \"version=${version}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Sync package.json version to tag\r\n        run: |\r\n          node -e \"\r\n            const fs = require('fs');\r\n            const p = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('package.json','utf8'));\r\n            p.version = '${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}';\r\n            fs.writeFileSync('package.json', JSON.stringify(p, null, 2) + '\\n');\r\n          \"\r\n\r\n      - name: Build all targets (bun cross-compilation)\r\n        run: bun run build:all\r\n\r\n      - name: Upload build artifacts\r\n        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          name: release-tarballs\r\n          path: |\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz.sha256\r\n          retention-days: 7\r\n\r\n  release:\r\n    needs: build\r\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Download build artifacts\r\n        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          name: release-tarballs\r\n          path: dist/\r\n\r\n      - name: Create GitHub Release\r\n        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2\r\n        with:\r\n          draft: false\r\n          prerelease: false\r\n          generate_release_notes: true\r\n          files: |\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz\r\n            dist/skvm-*.tar.gz.sha256\r\n\r\n  npm-publish:\r\n    needs: [build, release]\r\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\r\n    permissions:\r\n      contents: read\r\n      id-token:[REDACTED]\r\n    # Kill switch: publishing is enabled by default; set the\r\n    # ENABLE_NPM_PUBLISH repo variable to \"false\" to skip this job\r\n    # (e.g. during staged rollouts or if npm is broken).\r\n    if: ${{ vars.ENABLE_NPM_PUBLISH != 'false' }}\r\n    # Trusted Publishing: auth is via GitHub Actions OIDC JWT, not NPM_TOKEN.\r\n    # The `id-token:[REDACTED] permission at the top of this workflow issues the\r\n    # JWT; the npm side must have this workflow configured as a Trusted\r\n    # Publisher under @ipads-skvm/skvm's package settings. No secrets here.\r\n    steps:\r\n      - name: Checkout\r\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          submodules: false\r\n\r\n      - name: Setup Node\r\n        uses: actions/setup-node@v4\r\n        with:\r\n          # npm Trusted Publishing currently requires npm >= 11.5.1\r\n          # and Node >= 22.14.0; use a current LTS/runtime here.\r\n          node-version: \"24\"\r\n          registry-url: \"https://registry.npmjs.org\"\r\n\r\n      - name: Sync package.json version to tag\r\n        run: |\r\n          version=\"${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/v}\"\r\n          node -e \"\r\n            const fs = require('fs');\r\n            const p = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('package.json','utf8'));\r\n            p.version = '${version}';\r\n            fs.writeFileSync('package.json', JSON.stringify(p, null, 2) + '\\n');\r\n          \"\r\n\r\n      - name: Publish to npm\r\n        run: npm publish --access public\r\n",

        "content": "# GitHub Actions Security Review\n\n**Project/scope:** `./source`  \n**Review date:** 2026-10-03  \n**Mode:** Offline static review using the supplied `security-review` skill  \n**Files reviewed:** 1 (`source/release.yml`)  \n**Focus:** GitHub Actions token permissions and `uses:` action references only\n\n## Findings Summary\n\n| Severity | Count |\n|---|---:|\n| 🔴 CRITICAL | 0 |\n| 🟠 HIGH | 2 |\n| 🟡 MEDIUM | 0 |\n| 🔵 LOW | 0 |\n| ⚪ INFO | 0 |\n| **TOTAL** | **2** |\n\n**Dependency/CVE audit:** Not performed; outside the requested scope and unavailable for live verification in an offline review.  \n**Secrets scan:** No repository-wide secrets scan was performed; unrelated whole-repository audit duties are outside scope.  \n**Inventory:** See `workflow-inventory.json` for every workflow/job permission declaration and every action-reference occurrence with file/line evidence.\n\n## Scope Resolution and Inventory Summary\n\nThe supplied `source` directory contains one YAML file, `source/release.yml`. It is directly under `source/`, not under a conventional `.github/workflows/` directory, but it was treated as the supplied workflow artifact.\n\nThe workflow triggers on pushed tags matching `v*` (`source/release.yml:3-6`). Its workflow-level permission map declares:\n\n```yaml\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  id-token:[REDACTED]\n```\n\nEvidence: `source/release.yml:8-10`.\n\nPermission declaration states were kept distinct:\n\n| Job | Declaration state | Statically effective permissions | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `build` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:13-21`, inherited from `:8-10` |\n| `release` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:71-74`, inherited from `:8-10` |\n| `npm-publish` | **Explicit map** | `contents: read`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:99-101` |\n\nNo workflow or job uses an **explicitly empty** permission declaration such as `permissions: {}`. Omitted job permissions are not recorded as explicitly empty: `build` and `release` inherit the workflow-level map.\n\nEight `uses:` occurrences were found, comprising six unique references. Every reference uses a mutable major-version tag rather than a full commit SHA:\n\n- `actions/checkout@v4` — lines 23, 76, 112\n- `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` — line 28\n- `actions/upload-artifact@v4` — line 63\n- `actions/download-artifact@v4` — line 81\n- `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` — line 87\n- `actions/setup-node@v4` — line 117\n\n## Findings by Category\n\n### Authentication and Access Control: Workflow Token Permissions\n\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  \n🟠 **HIGH — Build and release jobs inherit broader token authority than each job requires**  \n**Confidence: HIGH**  \n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\n\n**Locations:**\n\n- Workflow permission map: `source/release.yml:8-10`\n- `build` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:13-21`\n- Dependency installation and build commands: `source/release.yml:36-60`\n- `release` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:71-74`\n- GitHub release operation: `source/release.yml:86-94`\n- Narrower `npm-publish` job map: `source/release.yml:99-101`\n\n**Relevant code:**\n\n```yaml\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  id-token:[REDACTED]\n\njobs:\n  build:\n    runs-on: macos-latest\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\n\n  release:\n    needs: build\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\n```\n\n**Risk:**\n\nThe `build` job inherits both `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED] while checking out code, invoking a third-party setup action, installing dependencies, and running project build commands. If any executed action, dependency lifecycle path, or build script were compromised, the job would have repository-content write authority and could request an OIDC token. The build steps shown do not demonstrate a need for either capability beyond ordinary source checkout.\n\nThe `release` job needs `contents: write` to create the GitHub release, but it also inherits `id-token:[REDACTED] even though no release step shown requests OIDC authentication. Granting OIDC token issuance to jobs that do not use it unnecessarily expands the credential surface.\n\nThe `npm-publish` job is better scoped: it explicitly declares `contents: read` and `id-token:[REDACTED] at lines 99-101.\n\n**Recommended fix (proposal only):**\n\nDeclare least-privilege permissions per job instead of granting both write capabilities at workflow scope.       "output": "# GitHub Actions Security Review\n\n**Project/scope:** `./source`  \n**Review date:** 2026-10-03  \n**Mode:** Offline static review using the supplied `security-review` skill  \n**Files reviewed:** 1 (`source/release.yml`)  \n**Focus:** GitHub Actions token permissions and `uses:` action references only\n\n## Findings Summary\n\n| Severity | Count |\n|---|---:|\n| 🔴 CRITICAL | 0 |\n| 🟠 HIGH | 2 |\n| 🟡 MEDIUM | 0 |\n| 🔵 LOW | 0 |\n| ⚪ INFO | 0 |\n| **TOTAL** | **2** |\n\n**Dependency/CVE audit:** Not performed; outside the requested scope and unavailable for live verification in an offline review.  \n**Secrets scan:** No repository-wide secrets scan was performed; unrelated whole-repository audit duties are outside scope.  \n**Inventory:** See `workflow-inventory.json` for every workflow/job permission declaration and every action-reference occurrence with file/line evidence.\n\n## Scope Resolution and Inventory Summary\n\nThe supplied `source` directory contains one YAML file, `source/release.yml`. It is directly under `source/`, not under a conventional `.github/workflows/` directory, but it was treated as the supplied workflow artifact.\n\nThe workflow triggers on pushed tags matching `v*` (`source/release.yml:3-6`). Its workflow-level permission map declares:\n\n```yaml\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  id-token:[REDACTED]\n```\n\nEvidence: `source/release.yml:8-10`.\n\nPermission declaration states were kept distinct:\n\n| Job | Declaration state | Statically effective permissions | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `build` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:13-21`, inherited from `:8-10` |\n| `release` | **Omitted; inherits workflow** | `contents: write`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:71-74`, inherited from `:8-10` |\n| `npm-publish` | **Explicit map** | `contents: read`, `id-token:[REDACTED]; unlisted scopes `none` | `source/release.yml:99-101` |\n\nNo workflow or job uses an **explicitly empty** permission declaration such as `permissions: {}`. Omitted job permissions are not recorded as explicitly empty: `build` and `release` inherit the workflow-level map.\n\nEight `uses:` occurrences were found, comprising six unique references. Every reference uses a mutable major-version tag rather than a full commit SHA:\n\n- `actions/checkout@v4` — lines 23, 76, 112\n- `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` — line 28\n- `actions/upload-artifact@v4` — line 63\n- `actions/download-artifact@v4` — line 81\n- `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` — line 87\n- `actions/setup-node@v4` — line 117\n\n## Findings by Category\n\n### Authentication and Access Control: Workflow Token Permissions\n\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  \n🟠 **HIGH — Build and release jobs inherit broader token authority than each job requires**  \n**Confidence: HIGH**  \n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\n\n**Locations:**\n\n- Workflow permission map: `source/release.yml:8-10`\n- `build` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:13-21`\n- Dependency installation and build commands: `source/release.yml:36-60`\n- `release` job with no job-level permission override: `source/release.yml:71-74`\n- GitHub release operation: `source/release.yml:86-94`\n- Narrower `npm-publish` job map: `source/release.yml:99-101`\n\n**Relevant code:**\n\n```yaml\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n  id-token:[REDACTED]\n\njobs:\n  build:\n    runs-on: macos-latest\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\n\n  release:\n    needs: build\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # permissions omitted: inherits both write scopes\n```\n\n**Risk:**\n\nThe `build` job inherits both `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED] while checking out code, invoking a third-party setup action, installing dependencies, and running project build commands. If any executed action, dependency lifecycle path, or build script were compromised, the job would have repository-content write authority and could request an OIDC token. The build steps shown do not demonstrate a need for either capability beyond ordinary source checkout.\n\nThe `release` job needs `contents: write` to create the GitHub release, but it also inherits `id-token:[REDACTED] even though no release step shown requests OIDC authentication. Granting OIDC token issuance to jobs that do not use it unnecessarily expands the credential surface.\n\nThe `npm-publish` job is better scoped: it explicitly declares `contents: read` and `id-token:[REDACTED] at lines 99-101.\n\n**Recommended fix (proposal only):**\n\nDeclare least-privilege permissions per job instead of granting both write capabilities at workflow scope. Based only on the visible steps, a likely structure is:\n\n```yaml\npermissions: {}\n\njobs:\n  build:\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n\n  release:\n    permissions:\n      contents: write\n\n  npm-publish:\n    permissions:\n      contents: read\n      id-token:[REDACTED]\n```\n\nValidate whether artifact upload/download behavior or repository policy requires any additional scope before adopting this proposal.\n\n**Reference:** GitHub Actions least-privilege `GITHUB_TOKEN` and OIDC permission design.\n\n**Self-verification:**\n\n- Re-read all three job declarations and the workflow-level map.\n- Confirmed `build` has no job-level `permissions` key between lines 13-70.\n- Confirmed `release` has no job-level `permissions` key between lines 71-95.\n- Confirmed `npm-publish` has an explicit override at lines 99-101.\n- Confirmed no visible `build` or `release` step explicitly consumes an OIDC token.\n- No upstream reusable workflow or composite action can change the fact that these jobs receive the declared token permissions; however, offline review cannot observe repository rules or runtime token use inside fetched actions.\n\n---\n\n### Supply-Chain Integrity: Action References\n\n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  \n🟠 **HIGH — All external actions are referenced by mutable major-version tags**  \n**Confidence: HIGH**  \n━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━\n\n**Locations:**\n\n- `actions/checkout@v4` — `source/release.yml:23`, `:76`, `:112`\n- `oven-sh/setup-bun@v2` — `source/release.yml:28`\n- `actions/upload-artifact@v4` — `source/release.yml:63`\n- `actions/download-artifact@v4` — `source/release.yml:81`\n- `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` — `source/release.yml:87`\n- `actions/setup-node@v4` — `source/release.yml:117`\n\n**Representative code:**\n\n```yaml\nuses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v2\nuses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2\nuses: actions/setup-node@v4\n```\n\n**Risk:**\n\nMajor-version tags are movable references. A future tag update or upstream compromise can change the code executed by this release workflow without a corresponding commit in this repository. The impact is elevated because:\n\n- `build` executes tagged actions while holding inherited `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED]\n- `release` executes tagged actions while holding inherited `contents: write` and `id-token:[REDACTED]\n- `npm-publish` executes tagged actions while able to request an OIDC token used for trusted npm publishing.\n\nA compromised action in these jobs could misuse the job's token, alter release inputs, tamper with artifacts available to later steps, or interfere with package publication.\n\n**Recommended fix (proposal only):**\n\nPin every external action to a reviewed full-length commit SHA, retaining the release tag in a comment for maintainability. Example form:\n\n```yaml\n- uses: actions/checkout@<reviewed-40-character-commit-sha> # v4\n```\n\nUse a controlled update mechanism to review and advance pinned SHAs. Exact SHAs are intentionally not proposed here because this was an offline review and no upstream reference resolution or authenticity verification was performed.\n\n**Reference:** GitHub Actions supply-chain hardening; immutable action pinning.\n\n**Self-verification:**\n\n- Enumerated every `uses:` key in the supplied file; there are eight occurrences and six unique references.\n- Confirmed each suffix is `@v2` or `@v4`, not a full commit SHA.\n- No local actions (`./path`) or reusable-workflow `uses:` references were present.\n- Did not infer that any referenced version is currently compromised or vulnerable; the finding concerns mutable reference integrity only.\n\n## Patch Proposals\n\n**Review each patch before applying.
